unknown region.

I'm settling on a literal reading of residual ties — any two beneficiaries

were actually distributed, since the text's definition doesn't condition on the 
handout outcome. I'm also confirming that the minimum only applies in the LR

I'm worried a fresh extract with more sites per event-region could cause the 
brute-force enumeration to blow up well past the time limit — a worst case like 
8 sites with 3 offers each would be far too many leaves to check. I'm weighing 
whether branch-and-bound could prune the search while still correctly counting 
ties among optimal solutions, since that's a harder requirement than just 
finding one best answer.

The key issue is the lexicographic key components don't move monotonically as I 
add offers — shortfall and relief shrink while the other components grow — so a 
naive pruning rule isn't sound. I need a valid lower bound, like assuming all 
remaining offers contribute their maximum relief to get the smallest possible 
shortfall, and only prune branches whose bound strictly exceeds the current 
best.

Given the small input size (at most 5 sites, 3 offers each) and the generous 
time budget, full enumeration with this conservative pruning on the first 
component is both safe and practical.

I can compute an upper bound on achievable relief at each node by taking, per 
interval, the maximum relief among remaining offers, suffix-summing these, and 
comparing against the best c1 found so far — if a subtree's bound can't beat it,
prune. This costs roughly O(intervals × candidates) per node but should still 
keep the search fast given the small branching factor.

Given the shipped run takes 0.9s against a 120s budget, and the realistic sizing
is around 5 sites with ≤3 offers each, I have massive headroom — even scaling to
8-9 sites would stay well within budget, and since the actual data consistently 
shows 5 sites per event-region, this bound should rarely even be necessary but 
is worth adding since it's cheap and always safe.

The spec's reassurance that a site has at most 3 candidate offers per event 
confirms plain enumeration with ceiling pruning is fine, so I'll skip riskier 
dominance pruning. I'm now re-checking schema details like event_count and the 
totals fields in §8.1/§8.2 to make sure my implementation matches exactly.

$ cd /app && python3 -c "
import sys, json, csv, collections, random
from fractions import Fraction
from decimal import Decimal, ROUND_HALF_EVEN
sys.path.insert(0,'/app/out')
import derive_report as dr
import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET

ex = dr.Extract('/app/data')
res,_,_ = dr.derive(ex)
# independent rate + credit using Decimal half-even
cards={}
for c in ET.parse('/app/data/rate_cards.xml').getroot().findall('card'):
    ov=c.find('override')
    cards[(c.get('event'),c.get('site'))]=(int(c.find('base').text), None if ov 
is None else int(ov.text), int(c.find('cap').text))
sites={}
for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/sites.csv')):
    sites[(r['event_id'],r['site_id'])]=r
ev={e['event_id']:e for e in json.load(open('/app/data/events.json'))}
adder={'C':0,'P':750}
random.seed(1)
chosen=[(k,oid) for k,v in res.items() for oid in v['selected_offer_ids']]
print('chosen offers',len(chosen))
bad=0; capb=0
for k,oid in chosen:
    off=ex.offers[oid]; s=sites[(off.event_id,off.site_id)]
    base,ovr,cap=cards[(off.event_id,off.site_id)]
    rate=(base if ovr is None else 
ovr)+adder[s['segment_code']]+ev[off.event_id]['uplift_millicents_per_kwh']
    if rate>cap: capb+=1
    eff=min(rate,cap)
    tot=0
    for t in range(ev[off.event_id]['interval_count']):
        cr=max(0,off.rel[t]-off.rec[t])
        amt=Decimal(cr*10*eff)/Decimal(1000*1000)
        tot+=int(amt.quantize(Decimal(1),rounding=ROUND_HALF_EVEN))
    # recompute with program path
    rate2=ex.sites[(off.event_id,off.site_id)]['rate']
    if rate2!=eff: bad+=1; print('rate mismatch',oid)
    got=0
    for t in range(ev[off.event_id]['interval_count']):
        cr=off.rel[t]-off.rec[t]
        if cr>0: got+=dr.div_half_even(cr*10*rate2,1000000)
    if got!=tot: bad+=1; print('credit mismatch',oid,got,tot)
print('mismatches',bad,'cap binding',capb)
# cross-check against written policy_audit
pa=json.load(open('/app/out/policy_audit.json'))
print('policy cap_binding_count',pa['cap_binding_count'])
tot_credit=sum(v['credit_cents'] for v in res.values())
print('total credit',tot_credit, 
json.load(open('/app/out/summary.json'))['credit_cents'])
"

import sys, json, csv, collections
sys.path.insert(0,'/app/out')
import derive_report as dr
ex=dr.Extract('/app/data'); res,ties,st=dr.derive(ex)

# --- independent allowance replay straight from the raw files ---
raw={}
for ln in open('/app/data/site_allowances.tsv'):
    if ln.startswith('#'): continue
    p=ln.rstrip('\n').split('\t')
    if p[0]=='site_id': continue
    raw[p[0]]=(int(p[1]), p[2] if len(p)>2 else '')
def root(s):
    while raw[s][1]: s=raw[s][1]
    return s
events=json.load(open('/app/data/events.json'))
import datetime
order=sorted(events,key=lambda 
e:(datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(e['dispatch_start']),))
# group equal instants, descending id
g=collections.defaultdict(list)
for e in events: 
g[datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(e['dispatch_start'])].append(e['event_id'])
season=[i for k in sorted(g) for i in sorted(g[k],reverse=True)]
assert season==ex.season_order
sites={}
for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/sites.csv')): 
sites[(r['event_id'],r['site_id'])]=r
state={}
for ln in open('/app/data/site_state.jsonl'):
    d=json.loads(ln); state[(d['event_id'],d['site_id'])]=d
offers=[r for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/offers.csv'))]
by_ev=collections.defaultdict(list)
for o in offers:
    if (o['event_id'],o['site_id']) in sites: by_ev[o['event_id']].append(o)
used=collections.Counter(); inel=0; blocked=0; cand=0
regmap={k.lower():v for k,v in ex.region_of_code.items()}
for eid in season:
    add=collections.Counter()
    for o in by_ev[eid]:
        if not state[(o['event_id'],o['site_id'])]['dispatch_authorized']:
            inel+=1; continue
        r=root(o['site_id'])
        if ex.offers[o['offer_id']].tot_rel > raw[r][0]-used[r]: blocked+=1; 
continue
        cand+=1
    for k,v in res.items():
        if k[0]!=eid: continue
        for oid in v['selected_offer_ids']:
            off=ex.offers[oid]; add[root(off.site_id)]+=off.tot_rel
    used.update(add)
print('independent: ineligible',inel,'blocked',blocked,'candidates',cand)
dq=json.load(open('/app/out/data_quality.json'))
print('program    : 
ineligible',dq['ineligible_offer_count'],'blocked',dq['allowance_blocked_offer_c
ount'],'candidates',json.load(open('/app/out/summary.json'))['candidate_offer_co
unt'])

# --- independent grants check for every event-region (claims/LR from raw) ---
wts={}
for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/recipient_weights.tsv'),delimiter='\t'):
    wts[(r['event_id'],r['site_id'])]=int(r['grant_weight'])
pa=json.load(open('/app/out/policy_audit.json'))
labels={(d['event_id'],d['site_id']):d['label'] for d in pa['site_labels']}
carry=collections.Counter(); bad=0; claims_branch=0
pools={}
for ln in open('/app/data/pools.txt'):
    if ln.startswith('#'): continue
    pools[(ln[0:10].strip(), regmap[ln[10:14].strip().lower()])]=int(ln[14:26])
for eid in season:
    for reg in ex.regions:
        v=res[(eid,reg)]
        F=max(0,pools.get((eid,reg),0)+carry[reg]-v['credit_cents'])
        bw=collections.Counter(); nrec=0
        for (e,s),site in sites.items():
            if e!=eid or regmap[site['region_code'].lower()]!=reg: continue
            if (e,s) not in wts: continue
            nrec+=1
            w=wts[(e,s)]*(2 if labels[(e,s)]=='CONTINUITY_WAIVED' else 1)
            bw[site['beneficiary_id']]+=w
        Wt=sum(bw.values())
        if nrec==0 or Wt==0:
            alloc=0; shares={}
        else:
            pos={b:w for b,w in bw.items() if w>0}
            cl={b:w*40 for b,w in pos.items()}
            if sum(cl.values())<=F:
                shares=cl; alloc=sum(cl.values()); claims_branch+=1
            else:
                shares=None; alloc=None
        if alloc is not None and (alloc!=v['grant_allocated_cents'] or 
F!=v['grant_funded_cents']):
            bad+=1
            if bad<4: print('grant 
mismatch',eid,reg,F,v['grant_funded_cents'],alloc,v['grant_allocated_cents'])
        if shares is not None:
            got={g['beneficiary_id']:g['cents'] for g in v['grants']}
            if got!={b:c for b,c in shares.items()}: bad+=1; print('share 
mismatch',eid,reg)
        carry[reg]=F-(alloc if alloc is not None else 
v['grant_allocated_cents'])
print('grant mismatches',bad,'claims-paid 
event-regions',claims_branch,'of',len(res))

$ cd /app && python3 -c "
import sys, time
sys.path.insert(0,'/app/out')
import derive_report as dr
class O:
    __slots__=('oid','rel','rec','bid','tot_rel','tot_rec','seg')
    def __init__(s,i,n):
        s.oid='OFF-%05d'%i; s.rel=[(i*7)%40+1]*n; s.rec=[(i*3)%20]*n
        s.bid=100+i; s.tot_rel=sum(s.rel); s.tot_rec=sum(s.rec); s.seg=i%2
for k in (5,7,8,9,10):
    opts=[[O(i*3+j,6) for j in range(3)] for i in range(k)]
    ic=6; tgt=[200]*ic; w=[1]*ic; rc=[10**9]*ic; cc=[10**9]*ic
    t=time.time()
    reps=50
    for _ in range(reps):
        dr.best_portfolio(ic,opts,tgt,w,rc,cc,[(0,500,2)],1,1,2)
    per=(time.time()-t)/reps
    print('sites=%d  4^k=%d  %.4fs per group -> %.1fs for 2000 
groups'%(k,4**k,per,per*2000))
"

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Interrupt benchmark

Reasoning:
Without ceiling pruning, timing jumps sharply between 7 and 8 sites per group, 
exceeding budget at 8. The shipped data caps at exactly 5 sites per group 
everywhere with heavy pruning evident in the optimizer timing, which makes me 
suspect the generator intentionally limits group size to stay within performance
bounds, and a fresh extract with more sites per region could risk being too 
slow.

To be safe, I want a provably-correct lower-bound pruning strategy instead of 
relying on that assumption. I'm working through a branch-and-bound scheme on the
lexicographic key, where at each decision node I'd compute optimistic lower 
bounds for each component of the cost tuple — relief per interval, relief per 
segment, and the bid/recovery weighted cost — and prune any branch whose bound 
already exceeds the best known solution lexicographically.

But I'm realizing the per-component lower-bound vector isn't actually valid for 
lexicographic comparison: different leaves can achieve the minimum of different 
components, so a leaf's true key could still be smaller than what the 
component-wise bound suggests, meaning this pruning approach would risk cutting 
off the actual optimal solution.

This confirms the component-wise lower-bound comparison is a valid prune: 
pruning only occurs when lb strictly exceeds the current best, so any leaf tied 
with or better than best survives. Since best only improves (decreases) over 
time, previously valid prunes remain safe as the search progresses.

For tie counting, if a strictly better key appears later, I reset the tie count 
rather than accumulating stale ties. Now I'm working out how to compute the 
bounds efficiently by precomputing suffix maximums — one array tracking the best
possible relief contribution from each site onward, and another tracking the 
best segment-specific relief, since all offers at a given site share the same 
segment.

From these I can assemble several lower bounds: one from unmet target weights, 
one from unmet floor terms using segment relief, one from bid/record weighting, 
one penalizing excess relief over target, and a trivial count-based bound. Each 
bound costs roughly O(interval count + segment count) to evaluate per node, 
similar to leaf evaluation, and since internal nodes are far fewer than leaves 
this should stay efficient overall.

The real question is whether this extra complexity pays off. The bid/record 
bound looks especially strong — once the partial bid cost alone exceeds the 
current best while the other components already match optimal, I can prune 
outright. But that pruning condition only triggers in the specific case where 
the first two bounds equal the best values and the third exceeds it.

Since the baseline (0) is usually achievable for most nodes, that third-bound 
pruning should kick in fairly often in practice. I'm wary of introducing a 
subtle bug though, so I want to validate carefully: confirm all nine shipped 
files still produce byte-identical output, check the synthetic test with its 
2000 hand-verified event-regions, and run a randomized stress comparison between
the pruned and brute-force unpruned versions to catch any discrepancy. Given the
robustness this buys for meeting the time budget on larger extracts, I think 
it's worth implementing — I'll proceed carefully with the plan for 
best_portfolio.

I'm computing suffix arrays that track, for each position going backward, the 
maximum achievable relevance per interval-constraint type and the maximum total 
relevance per segment, so that during the search walk I can bound the remaining 
potential before deciding each site and prune branches early.

Now I'm reorganizing the walk function so it checks the bound against the best 
known key before recursing further, folding the multiple lower-bound 
computations (lb1 through lb5) into shared loops over the constraint ranges to 
avoid redundant passes.

I realize that at the leaf node, the suffix relief and segment arrays are all 
zero, meaning the computed bound exactly equals the true key — so I can 
eliminate the separate exact-evaluation code by just using the bound function at
i==n too, simplifying the recursion considerably.

Still writing lower-bound logic...

Now I'm pinning down the pruning condition at the leaf node: if a key is 
strictly worse than the current best, I bail out early, otherwise I need to 
decide whether it replaces or ties the best solution found so far before 
continuing the recursive walk over remaining choices.

I'm confirming the lower bounds are sound since bids and recovery values are 
non-negative, so partial sums only increase as I add more offers, and that the 
recursion correctly starts from the empty portfolio as its first candidate 
before exploring further.

I also need to verify the pruning logic preserves correct tie-counting: a branch
gets pruned only when its lower bound exceeds the best value known at that time,
and since the final best can only be equal to or worse than that interim best, 
any pruned branch's leaves are guaranteed strictly worse than the final optimum 
— so no tied-best leaves get discarded.

Now I should implement this and check for byte-identical output, keeping in mind
the suffix array setup adds negligible overhead per group. Before implementing, 
I should stop the benchmark that's still running on sites=10.

Arguments:

  old_str: 
    def best_portfolio(ic, opts, target, weight, rceil, cceil, floor_terms, cw, 
rw, n_seg):
        """Enumerate feasible portfolios; return (selected offer ids, tie 
count).
    
        A portfolio holds at most one candidate offer per site. Adding an offer 
never
        lowers relief or recovery, so a branch that breaks a ceiling is pruned 
whole.
        """
        n = len(opts)
        relief = [0] * ic
        recov = [0] * ic
        segrel = [0] * (n_seg + 1)
        chosen = []
        rng = range(ic)
        best_key = None
        best_sel = ()
        tie_count = 0
    
        def walk(i, bid, rec_tot, nsel):
            nonlocal best_key, best_sel, tie_count
            if i == n:
                c1 = 0
                c4 = 0
                for t in rng:
                    d = target[t] - relief[t]
                    if d > 0:
                        c1 += weight[t] * d
                    elif d < 0:
                        c4 -= d
                c2 = 0
                for sidx, fticks, pw in floor_terms:
                    d = fticks - segrel[sidx]
                    if d > 0:
                        c2 += pw * d
                key = (c1, c2, cw * bid + rw * rec_tot, c4, nsel)
                if best_key is None or key < best_key:
                    best_key = key
                    best_sel = tuple(sorted(chosen))
                    tie_count = 1
                elif key == best_key:
                    tie_count += 1
                    cand = tuple(sorted(chosen))
                    if cand > best_sel:
                        best_sel = cand
                return
            walk(i + 1, bid, rec_tot, nsel)

  new_str: 
    def best_portfolio(ic, opts, target, weight, rceil, cceil, floor_terms, cw, 
rw, n_seg):
        """Enumerate feasible portfolios; return (selected offer ids, tie 
count).
    
        A portfolio holds at most one candidate offer per site. Adding an offer 
never
        lowers relief or recovery, so a branch that breaks a ceiling is pruned 
whole.
    
        Each component of the objective is also bounded from below over a 
branch: the
        sites still to decide can add at most their largest relief, and cost, 
recovery
        and offer count only grow. Bounding the components one by one bounds the
whole
        key in lexicographic order, so a branch whose bound is above the best 
key so
        far holds no optimal and no tied portfolio and is dropped.
        """
        n = len(opts)
        relief = [0] * ic
        recov = [0] * ic
        segrel = [0] * (n_seg + 1)
        chosen = []
        rng = range(ic)
        best_key = None
        best_sel = ()
        tie_count = 0
    
        suf_rel = [[0] * ic for _ in range(n + 1)]
        suf_seg = [[0] * (n_seg + 1) for _ in range(n + 1)]
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            nxt_rel = suf_rel[i + 1]
            cur_rel = suf_rel[i]
            for t in rng:
                cur_rel[t] = nxt_rel[t] + max(off.rel[t] for off in opts[i])
            suf_seg[i] = list(suf_seg[i + 1])
            suf_seg[i][opts[i][0].seg] += max(off.tot_rel for off in opts[i])
    
        def walk(i, bid, rec_tot, nsel):
            nonlocal best_key, best_sel, tie_count
            lb1 = 0
            lb4 = 0
            sr = suf_rel[i]
            for t in rng:
                r = relief[t]
                tg = target[t]
                d = tg - r - sr[t]
                if d > 0:
                    lb1 += weight[t] * d
                elif r > tg:
                    lb4 += r - tg
            lb2 = 0
            ss = suf_seg[i]
            for sidx, fticks, pw in floor_terms:
                d = fticks - segrel[sidx] - ss[sidx]
                if d > 0:
                    lb2 += pw * d
            key = (lb1, lb2, cw * bid + rw * rec_tot, lb4, nsel)
            if best_key is not None and key > best_key:
                return
            if i == n:
                # with no site left to decide the bound is the portfolio's own 
key
                if best_key is None or key < best_key:
                    best_key = key
                    best_sel = tuple(sorted(chosen))
                    tie_count = 1
                else:
                    tie_count += 1
                    cand = tuple(sorted(chosen))
                    if cand > best_sel:
                        best_sel = cand
                return
            walk(i + 1, bid, rec_tot, nsel)

import sys, random, itertools, time
sys.path.insert(0,'/app/out')
import derive_report as dr

class O:
    __slots__=('oid','rel','rec','bid','tot_rel','tot_rec','seg')

def mk(oid,ic,seg,rnd):
    o=O(); o.oid=oid
    o.rel=[rnd.choice([0,0,1,5,20,50,120]) for _ in range(ic)]
    o.rec=[rnd.choice([0,0,3,10,40]) for _ in range(ic)]
    o.bid=rnd.choice([0,10,10,250,900]); o.seg=seg
    o.tot_rel=sum(o.rel); o.tot_rec=sum(o.rec)
    return o

def ref(ic,opts,tgt,w,rc,cc,ft,cw,rw,ns):
    best=None; sel=None; cnt=0
    for combo in itertools.product(*[[None]+g for g in opts]):
        ch=[o for o in combo if o]
        rel=[sum(o.rel[t] for o in ch) for t in range(ic)]
        rec=[sum(o.rec[t] for o in ch) for t in range(ic)]
        if any(rel[t]>rc[t] or rec[t]>cc[t] for t in range(ic)): continue
        sg=[0]*(ns+1)
        for o in ch: sg[o.seg]+=o.tot_rel
        k=(sum(w[t]*max(0,tgt[t]-rel[t]) for t in range(ic)),
           sum(pw*max(0,f-sg[s]) for s,f,pw in ft),
           cw*sum(o.bid for o in ch)+rw*sum(rec),
           sum(max(0,rel[t]-tgt[t]) for t in range(ic)), len(ch))
        ids=tuple(sorted(o.oid for o in ch))
        if best is None or k<best: best,sel,cnt=k,ids,1
        elif k==best:
            cnt+=1
            if ids>sel: sel=ids
    return sel,cnt

rnd=random.Random(7); bad=0; ties=0
for trial in range(4000):
    ic=rnd.choice([1,2,3,6]); nsites=rnd.randint(0,5); ns=2
    opts=[]
    for i in range(nsites):
        k=rnd.randint(1,3); seg=rnd.randrange(ns)
        opts.append([mk('OF%02d%d'%(i,j),ic,seg,rnd) for j in range(k)])
    tgt=[rnd.choice([0,0,30,100,300]) for _ in range(ic)]
    w=[rnd.choice([0,1,1,4]) for _ in range(ic)]

    cc=[rnd.choice([0,20,80,10**6]) for _ in range(ic)]
    ft=[(s,f,pw) for s,f,pw in ((s,rnd.choice([0,50,200]),rnd.choice([0,2,7])) 
for s in range(ns)) if f>0 and pw>0]

,1,2]),ns)

    cw,rw=0,0
    # redo with explicit weights so both see the same
for trial in range(4000):

    cw=rnd.choice([0,1,3]); rw=rnd.choice([0,1,2])

for s in range(ns)) if f>0 and pw>0]

    exp=ref(ic,opts,tgt,w,rc,cc,ft,cw,rw,ns)
    if got!=exp:
        bad+=1
        if bad<4: print('MISMATCH',got,exp)
    if exp[1]>1: ties+=1
print('random trials 4000, mismatches',bad,'trials with ties',ties)

import derive_report as dr
class O:
    __slots__=('oid','rel','rec','bid','tot_rel','tot_rec','seg')
def mk(oid,ic,seg,rnd):

    o.rel=[rnd.choice([0,0,1,5,20,50,120]) for _ in range(ic)]
    o.rec=[rnd.choice([0,0,3,10,40]) for _ in range(ic)]
    o.bid=rnd.choice([0,10,10,250,900]); o.seg=seg

    return o

           sum(max(0,rel[t]-tgt[t]) for t in range(ic)), len(ch))

        if best is None or k<best: best,sel,cnt=k,ids,1
        elif k==best:
            cnt+=1
            if ids>sel: sel=ids
    return sel,cnt

Message from Agent ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Done. All nine reports are written to `/app/out`, produced by 
`/app/out/derive_report.py` (stdlib only, ~1 s per run).

## What the program does

**Parsing** (one `Extract` class): `program.ini` via `configparser` (codes 
matched case-insensitively, region/segment *names* used as the join key 
everywhere), `events.json`, the CSV/TSV tables, the two fixed-width files by the
exact character columns, `site_state.jsonl`, `rate_cards.xml`, and the two 
comment-headed TSVs. Unknown-region `sites.csv` rows are skipped and their lines
in the four dependent files ignored; orphan offers, and the three profile-row 
kinds in their stated order, are skipped and counted. Every Wh value becomes 
ticks (half up) the moment it is read from its record.

**Per site row**: compliance label by rank (`SAFETY_HOLD` → … → `CLEAR`, a 
waiver valid only when `VALID` + signed `Y` + critical + `public`), and the rate
= base-or-`override` + segment adder + event uplift, with the card's cap binding
that final rate.

**Season loop** in the order the `site_allowances.tsv` comment sets — by 
`dispatch_start` instant, equal instants by descending `event_id` — because both
the allowance draws and the grant carry are sequential. Per event-region: 
candidates (authorised, minus those exceeding what remains of the allowance 
their site's `draws_from` root holds), then the best portfolio, then credits 
(half-to-even per interval), then `F = max(0, pool + carry − credits)`, then the
apportionment (claims paid, else largest remainder with the minimum-grant 
re-share), whose unallocated cents carry to the same region's next event.

**Optimisation** is exact: a depth-first enumeration of portfolios with at most 
one offer per site. Breaking a ceiling prunes a branch whole (relief/recovery 
only grow), and each objective component is bounded below over a branch — 
bounding components one at a time bounds the lexicographic key, so a branch 
above the best key so far can hold neither an optimal nor a tied portfolio. 
Remaining ties are broken by the greatest sorted offer-id tuple, and the tied 
portfolios are counted for `tie_decisions`.

## How I checked it

- **Parsers**: every `field_sum` in `export_manifest.tsv` reproduced exactly 
(offers, both profile columns, targets, weights, allowances, feeder ceilings, 
pools, floors, card bases) — which validates the fixed-width column offsets and 
the comment/header handling.
- **Optimiser**: an independent `itertools.product` brute force (no pruning) 
over all 2000 event-regions, replaying the season for allowances — identical 
selections and identical tie counts. Plus 4000 randomised groups against the 
same reference (30 of them with genuine ties): no mismatch.
- **Money**: rates and credits of all 4152 chosen offers recomputed 
independently with `Decimal(ROUND_HALF_EVEN)`; allowance blocking, 
ineligibility, candidate counts and all grant figures recomputed from the raw 
files through a separate code path. All agreed.
- **Synthetic extract** with hand-computed expectations for the branches the 
shipped data never reaches: portfolio tie and greatest-identifier tie-break, 
largest-remainder shares, minimum-grant re-share, residual cents and residual 
tie, `F` clamped to 0, carry across the season order, `draws_from` chains and 
allowance blocking, override + cap binding, half-up ticks and half-even credits,
unknown-region skipping, orphan/duplicate/out-of-range profile rows, region-code
aliasing, and a region with no site rows. All ~70 expectations hold (I confirmed
the test fails when an expectation is perturbed).
- **Output shape**: nine files, compact sorted-key JSON with exactly one 
trailing newline, no floats or booleans anywhere, every array in the specified 
order, region/segment totals equal to the summary totals, and a byte-identical 
rerun into a fresh (non-existent, nested) `OUT_DIR`.

One note on the shipped numbers: `tie_decisions` is empty and `residual_cents` 
is 0 because every pool there is large enough for the claims to be paid in full 
(1911 event-regions) or has no recipients (89), and no two feasible portfolios 
tie — the synthetic extract is what exercises those code paths. The grant carry 
accumulates by design, which is why `grant_funded_cents` far exceeds 
`grant_pool_cents`. `/app/AGENTS.md` records these readings for future sessions.
